import type { ShapeNode, StrokePattern } from './design'

/**
 * Vector shape geometry: an SVG path (`d`) for a shape's kind inside its w×h box. The renderer,
 * the code export and the board export all draw vectors from this, so they match. Pure.
 */

const r = (value: number) => Math.round(value * 100) / 100

/** Rounded SVG caps turn a very short dash into a dot. */
export function strokeDashArray(pattern: StrokePattern | undefined, width: number): string | undefined {
  if (width <= 0) return undefined
  if (pattern === 'dashed') return `${3 * width} ${2 * width}`
  if (pattern === 'dotted') return `0.01 ${2 * width}`
  return undefined
}

/** The shape kinds drawn as an SVG path rather than a CSS box. */
export const VECTOR_KINDS = ['arrow', 'polygon', 'star', 'path'] as const
export function isVectorShape(node: ShapeNode): boolean {
  return (VECTOR_KINDS as readonly string[]).includes(node.kind)
}

/** Regular polygon points inscribed in the box (first vertex at the top). */
export function polygonPoints(sides: number, w: number, h: number): Array<[number, number]> {
  const n = Math.max(3, Math.min(24, Math.round(sides)))
  const out: Array<[number, number]> = []
  for (let i = 0; i < n; i += 1) {
    const angle = -Math.PI / 2 + (i * 2 * Math.PI) / n
    out.push([r(w / 2 + (w / 2) * Math.cos(angle)), r(h / 2 + (h / 2) * Math.sin(angle))])
  }
  return out
}

/** Star points (outer and inner alternating, first point at the top). */
export function starPoints(points: number, innerRatio: number, w: number, h: number): Array<[number, number]> {
  const n = Math.max(3, Math.min(24, Math.round(points)))
  const inner = Math.max(0.1, Math.min(0.95, innerRatio))
  const out: Array<[number, number]> = []
  for (let i = 0; i < n * 2; i += 1) {
    const angle = -Math.PI / 2 + (i * Math.PI) / n
    const k = i % 2 === 0 ? 1 : inner
    out.push([r(w / 2 + (w / 2) * k * Math.cos(angle)), r(h / 2 + (h / 2) * k * Math.sin(angle))])
  }
  return out
}

const pathOf = (points: Array<[number, number]>, closed: boolean) =>
  points.map(([x, y], i) => `${i === 0 ? 'M' : 'L'}${r(x)} ${r(y)}`).join(' ') + (closed ? ' Z' : '')

/**
 * A path whose segment i (point i to i + 1; with `closed`, the last back to the first) is a cubic
 * curve when `curves[i]` gives its two control points (shares of the box), else a line.
 */
export function curvedPathOf(
  points: Array<[number, number]>,
  curves: ReadonlyArray<readonly [number, number, number, number] | null>,
  closed: boolean,
  w: number,
  h: number,
): string {
  if (points.length === 0) return ''
  const parts = [`M${r(points[0][0])} ${r(points[0][1])}`]
  const segments = closed ? points.length : points.length - 1
  for (let i = 0; i < segments; i += 1) {
    const [x, y] = points[(i + 1) % points.length]
    const curve = curves[i]
    parts.push(curve ? `C${r(curve[0] * w)} ${r(curve[1] * h)} ${r(curve[2] * w)} ${r(curve[3] * h)} ${r(x)} ${r(y)}` : `L${r(x)} ${r(y)}`)
  }
  return parts.join(' ') + (closed ? ' Z' : '')
}

/**
 * The SVG path of a vector shape in its w×h box, plus whether it is filled (closed) or only
 * stroked. An arrow runs left to right through the box's middle with its head at the end.
 */
export function shapeGeometry(node: ShapeNode, w: number, h: number): { d: string; closed: boolean } {
  switch (node.kind) {
    case 'polygon':
      return { d: pathOf(polygonPoints(node.sides ?? 6, w, h), true), closed: true }
    case 'star':
      return { d: pathOf(starPoints(node.sides ?? 5, node.innerRatio ?? 0.5, w, h), true), closed: true }
    case 'path': {
      const points = (node.vertices ?? [[0, 0], [1, 1]]).map(([x, y]) => [x * w, y * h] as [number, number])
      if (!node.curves?.some(Boolean)) return { d: pathOf(points, node.closed === true), closed: node.closed === true }
      return { d: curvedPathOf(points, node.curves, node.closed === true, w, h), closed: node.closed === true }
    }
    case 'arrow': {
      const stroke = node.strokeWidth ?? 2
      const head = Math.min(w / 2, Math.max(8, stroke * 4))
      const mid = h / 2
      return {
        d: `M0 ${r(mid)} L${r(w)} ${r(mid)} M${r(w - head)} ${r(mid - head / 2)} L${r(w)} ${r(mid)} L${r(w - head)} ${r(mid + head / 2)}`,
        closed: false,
      }
    }
    default:
      return { d: `M0 0 L${r(w)} 0 L${r(w)} ${r(h)} L0 ${r(h)} Z`, closed: true }
  }
}

/**
 * A pen path from board px points (and per-segment cubic control points, also board px): its box
 * at (x, y), and the points and controls as shares of that box, which is how a path shape stores
 * them. The editor's pen and the agent's addPath both build paths with this.
 */
export function pathShapeFromPoints(
  points: Array<[number, number]>,
  curves: Array<[number, number, number, number] | null> | undefined,
  taken: ReadonlySet<string>,
  id: string,
): ShapeNode {
  const minX = Math.min(...points.map(([x]) => x))
  const minY = Math.min(...points.map(([, y]) => y))
  const w = Math.max(1, Math.max(...points.map(([x]) => x)) - minX)
  const h = Math.max(1, Math.max(...points.map(([, y]) => y)) - minY)
  const share = (value: number, from: number, size: number) => Math.round(((value - from) / size) * 1000) / 1000
  const clampShare = (value: number) => Math.min(2, Math.max(-1, value))
  let nodeId = id
  for (let n = 2; taken.has(nodeId); n += 1) nodeId = `${id}-${n}`
  return {
    type: 'shape',
    id: nodeId,
    kind: 'path',
    x: Math.round(minX),
    y: Math.round(minY),
    w: Math.round(w),
    h: Math.round(h),
    vertices: points.map(([x, y]) => [share(x, minX, w), share(y, minY, h)] as [number, number]),
    ...(curves?.some(Boolean)
      ? {
          curves: curves.map((curve) =>
            curve ? ([clampShare(share(curve[0], minX, w)), clampShare(share(curve[1], minY, h)), clampShare(share(curve[2], minX, w)), clampShare(share(curve[3], minY, h))] as [number, number, number, number]) : null,
          ),
        }
      : {}),
  }
}

/**
 * Pure edits and geometry for a slide's free-position elements (text boxes, shapes, lines,
 * arrows, images drawn above the layout). Shared by the editor (drag, resize, rotate, snap,

import {

  LIMITS,
  type Deck,
  type DeckAspect,
  type ElementType,
  type Slide,
  type SlideElement,

export type Box = { x: number; y: number; w: number; h: number }

const clamp = (value: number, min: number, max: number) => Math.max(min, Math.min(max, value))
const round = (value: number) => Math.round(value * 10) / 10

/** A rotation in (-180, 180], rounded to a tenth of a degree; 0 means none. */
export function normaliseRotation(degrees: number): number {
  if (!Number.isFinite(degrees)) return 0
  let value = degrees % 360
  if (value > 180) value -= 360
  if (value <= -180) value += 360
  return round(value) === 0 ? 0 : round(value)
}

/** A box kept inside the schema's bounds, rounded to a tenth of a pixel. */
export function clampBox(box: Box): Box {
  const w = clamp(round(box.w), ELEMENT_BOUNDS.minSize, ELEMENT_BOUNDS.maxSize)
  const h = clamp(round(box.h), ELEMENT_BOUNDS.minSize, ELEMENT_BOUNDS.maxSize)
  return {
    x: clamp(round(box.x), ELEMENT_BOUNDS.min, ELEMENT_BOUNDS.max),
    y: clamp(round(box.y), ELEMENT_BOUNDS.min, ELEMENT_BOUNDS.max),
    w,
    h,
  }
}

export function uniqueElementId(slide: Slide, base: string): string {
  const used = new Set((slide.elements ?? []).map((element) => element.id))

- File `common/src/desktop-cloud/artifact-kinds/whiteboard-route.ts` (2 matching lines):
```
const CACHE_SIZE = 2_000

function cacheKey(a: RouteEnd, b: RouteEnd, obstacles: readonly Rect[]): string {
  let key = `${a.point.x},${a.point.y},${a.side ?? ''}|${b.point.x},${b.point.y},${b.side ?? ''}`
  for (const r of obstacles) key += `|${r.x},${r.y},${r.w},${r.h}`
  return key
}

/**
 * A right-angled path from `a` to `b` around `obstacles`, as its corner points from `a.point` to
 * `b.point` inclusive, or null when there is none (or the search would be too big). The path
 * leaves `a` along `a.side` and arrives at `b` heading into `b.side` (it never leaves or arrives
 * backwards). Obstacles that already hold either end inside their margin are ignored, so a
 * crowded end still routes.
 */
export function routeOrthogonal(a: RouteEnd, b: RouteEnd, obstacles: readonly Rect[]): Point[] | null {
  const key = cacheKey(a, b, obstacles)
  if (cache.has(key)) {
    const hit = cache.get(key)!
    // Refresh its place in the LRU order.
    cache.delete(key)
    cache.set(key, hit)
    return hit
  }
  const result = search(a, b, obstacles)
  cache.set(key, result)
  if (cache.size > CACHE_SIZE) cache.delete(cache.keys().next().value!)
  return result
}

function search(a: RouteEnd, b: RouteEnd, obstacles: readonly Rect[]): Point[] | null {
  const blocks = obstacles
    .map((r) => inflateBy(r, ROUTE_MARGIN))
    .filter((r) => !strictlyInside(a.point, r) && !strictlyInside(b.point, r))
  // A frame one margin outside everything, so a route can always go round the outside.
  let fx1 = Math.min(a.point.x, b.point.x)
  let fy1 = Math.min(a.point.y, b.point.y)
  let fx2 = Math.max(a.point.x, b.point.x)
  let fy2 = Math.max(a.point.y, b.point.y)
  for (const r of blocks) {
    fx1 = Math.min(fx1, r.x)
    fy1 = Math.min(fy1, r.y)
    fx2 = Math.max(fx2, r.x + r.w)
    fy2 = Math.max(fy2, r.y + r.h)
  }
  const xsRaw = [a.point.x, b.point.x, (a.point.x + b.point.x) / 2, fx1 - ROUTE_MARGIN, fx2 + ROUTE_MARGIN]
  const ysRaw = [a.point.y, b.point.y, (a.point.y + b.point.y) / 2, fy1 - ROUTE_MARGIN, fy2 + ROUTE_MARGIN]
```

/**
 * render_artifact: a picture of an artifact, drawn by the user's open workspace tab.
 *
 * The run inserts one `artifact_render` event (the request below). A hosted workspace tab that
 * is watching the run live renders the kind's bare read-only view offscreen, rasterises it and
 * posts the PNG to `POST runs/:runId/renders/:eventId/:revision` (raw bytes), or posts
 * `.../unavailable` with a reason. The bytes wait under the run's own object prefix only until
 * the run reads (and deletes) them or the request expires; they are never an artifact asset, so
 * they never show in the asset list or count against its quota. Nothing answers in time: the
 * tool tells the model no render is available and to use the textual checks instead.
 */
…
const w = Math.max(1, contentWidth)
  const h = Math.max(1, contentHeight)
  const outWidth = Math.round(Math.min(ARTIFACT_RENDER_MAX_WIDTH, Math.max(ARTIFACT_RENDER_MIN_WIDTH, width)))
  const scale = outWidth / w
  const maxHeight = Math.max(1, Math.floor(ARTIFACT_RENDER_MAX_PIXELS / outWidth))
  const fullHeight = Math.max(1, Math.round(h * scale))
  const height = Math.min(fullHeight, maxHeight)
  return { width: outWidth, height, scale, cropHeight: Math.min(h, height / scale), cut: fullHeight > maxHeight }
}

/**
 * The next scale to try when an encoded picture came out `bytes` long against `limit`: area,
 * and so roughly bytes, goes with the square of the scale, with a margin so one retry usually
 * fits. Null once it fits, or when another try would drop under the minimum width.
 */
export function shrinkForBytes(bytes: number, limit: number, width: number): number | null {
  if (bytes <= limit) return null
  const factor = Math.sqrt(limit / bytes) * 0.9
  const next = Math.floor(width * Math.min(factor, 0.9))
  return next >= ARTIFACT_RENDER_MIN_WIDTH / 2 ? next / width : null
}

/**
 * A PNG's size from its IHDR chunk, or null when the bytes are not a PNG. The server checks
 * this itself rather than trusting what a client says it drew.

```
import { describe, expect, test } from 'bun:test'
import { ARTIFACT_KINDS } from './artifacts'
import { framedQuote } from './artifact-quote-frame'
import { artifactMention, artifactMentionKinds, readArtifactMentions } from './artifact-mentions'
describe('artifact mention tokens', () => {
  test('each selected format survives a plain prompt round trip', () => {
    for (const kind of ARTIFACT_KINDS) {
      const token = artifactMention(kind)
      const prompt = `Please use ${token} for this request.`
      expect(readArtifactMentions(prompt)).toEqual([{
        start: 11,
        end: 11 + token.length,
        text: token,
        kind,
        label: token.slice(2, token.indexOf(']')),
      }])
      expect(artifactMentionKinds(prompt)).toEqual([kind])
    }
  })
  test('plain aliases, mismatched labels and malformed tokens remain ordinary text', () => {
    expect(artifactMentionKinds('@docs @design user@docs.com path/@sheets.ts')).toEqual([])
    expect(readArtifactMentions('@[Docs](artifact:design) @[Design](artifact:unknown) @[Docs](artifact:doc')).toEqual([])
  })
  test('formats are deduplicated in selection order and restricted to the offered kinds', () => {
    const prompt = `${artifactMention('sheet')} ${artifactMention('doc')} ${artifactMention('sheet')}`
    expect(artifactMentionKinds(prompt)).toEqual(['sheet', 'doc'])
    expect(artifactMentionKinds(prompt, ['doc'])).toEqual(['doc'])
    expect(artifactMentionKinds(prompt, [])).toEqual([])
  })
  test('raw chips remain readable in examples without routing quoted content as a request', () => {
    const token = artifactMention('design')
    const prompt = [
      `An inline example: \`${token}\`.`,
      `Another example: \`\`${token}\`\`.`,
      `> ${token}`,
      `    ${token}`,
      '```markdown',
      token,
      '```',
      framedQuote({ source: `${token} template`, text: token, request: 'Summarize this example.' }),
      `Please write ${artifactMention('doc')} now.`,
    ].join('\n')

 /**
  * The chart spec shared by artifact kinds: the JSON a Docs ```chart block holds,
  * and the `chart` of a slide. Pure (zod only), so the server validates a body
  * with exactly the rules the app draws by.
  *
  *   { "type": "bar" | "line" | "area", "title"?: string, "x": "<row key for the x axis>",
  *     "series": [{ "key": "<row key>", "label": "<legend label>" }],   // 1-8, unique, not x
  *     "data": [{ "<x>": "Week 1", "<series key>": 40 }, ...] }           // 1-500 rows
  *
  * Every row has the x key; every value is a string or a finite number, and
- * series values are numbers. It mirrors `parseChartSpec` (freebuff/web doc/
- * DocChart.tsx) rule for rule and is never looser: it also rejects unknown keys
- * on a series entry. `slides-model.test.ts` checks both agree.
+ * series values are numbers. Docs strips legacy metadata on series entries before
+ * validating fenced JSON with this schema. Stored slide charts use the strict schema directly.
  *
  * Sheet charts (a range of cells) and data charts (a dataset's columns) are not
  * this spec: sheet.ts shares `CHART_TYPES`, data.ts the series limit.
  */
 
 import { z } from 'zod/v4'
 
 export const CHART_TYPES = ['bar', 'line', 'area'] as const
 export type ChartSpecType = (typeof CHART_TYPES)[number]
 
 export const CHART_LIMITS = { series: 8, rows: 500 } as const
 
 export type ChartSpec = {
   type: ChartSpecType
   title?: string
   /** The key in each row that labels the x axis. */
   x: string
   series: Array<{ key: string; label: string }>
   data: Array<Record<string, string | number>>
 }
 
 const chartCell = z.union([z.string(), z.number().finite()])

 // ---- Formatting
 
 const cache = new Map<string, Intl.NumberFormat>()
 function numberFormat(key: string, options: Intl.NumberFormatOptions): Intl.NumberFormat {
   let format = cache.get(key)
   if (!format) {
     format = new Intl.NumberFormat(LOCALE, options)
     cache.set(key, format)
   }
   return format
 }
 
 function trimNumber(value: number, digits: number): string {
   return numberFormat(`n${digits}`, { maximumFractionDigits: digits }).format(value)
 }
 
 /** 0.1234 → "12.3%". */
 export function formatPercent(fraction: number): string {
   return `${trimNumber(fraction * 100, 1)}%`
 }
 
-export function formatValue(value: number | null | undefined, format: ValueFormat, currency = 'USD'): string {
+export function formatValue(value: number | null | undefined, format: ValueFormat, currency = 'USD', percentDigits?: number): string {
   if (value === null || value === undefined || !Number.isFinite(value)) return EMPTY
   switch (format) {
     case 'integer':
       return numberFormat('int', { maximumFractionDigits: 0 }).format(value)
     case 'compact':
       if (Math.abs(value) < 1000) return trimNumber(value, Math.abs(value) < 10 ? 2 : 1)
       return numberFormat('compact', { notation: 'compact', maximumFractionDigits: 1 }).format(value)
     case 'currency': {
       const abs = Math.abs(value)
       if (abs >= 100_000) {
         return numberFormat(`cc-${currency}`, {
           style: 'currency',
           currency,
           notation: 'compact',
           maximumFractionDigits: 1,
         }).format(value)
       }
       const digits = abs >= 1000 || Number.isInteger(value) ? 0 : 2
       return numberFormat(`c${digits}-${currency}`, {
         style: 'currency',
         currency,
         minimumFractionDigits: digits,
         maximumFractionDigits: digits,
       }).format(value)
     }
     case 'percent':
-      return formatPercent(value)
+      return percentDigits === undefined ? formatPercent(value) : `${trimNumber(value * 100, percentDigits)}%`
     case 'number':
       return trimNumber(value, Math.abs(value) >= 100 ? 0 : 2)
   }
 }

       break
     }

 export function dashboardReadout(stored: Dashboard, now: number, limit = READOUT_LIMIT): string {
   // Calculated columns are computed here exactly as the page computes them.
   const dashboard = materializeDashboard(stored)
   const state = defaultFilterState(dashboard)
   const lines: string[] = []

   lines.push(checks.length ? `Checks: ${checks.join('; ')}.` : 'Checks: nothing to flag.')
   const fresh = dashboard.datasets.map((d) => {
     const f = datasetFreshness(dashboard, d, now)
     const recipe = d.refresh ? `, ${d.refresh.how ? `${d.refresh.how.kind} ` : ''}recipe${d.refresh.staleAfterHours ? ` (stale after ${d.refresh.staleAfterHours}h)` : ''}` : ', no recipe'
     const change = d.lastChange
       ? `, last change ${d.lastChange.at}: +${d.lastChange.added} -${d.lastChange.removed} ~${d.lastChange.changed} =${d.lastChange.unchanged}${d.lastChange.keyed ? '' : ' (unkeyed)'}`
       : ''
     const run = d.lastRun ? `, last run ${d.lastRun.status} ${d.lastRun.at}` : ''
     const mark = certification(d) === 'valid' ? ', certified' : certification(d) === 'void' ? ', certificate void' : ''
     const edited = d.edited ? `, EDITED by ${d.edited.by}` : ''
     return `${d.id} ${f.fetchedAt ? `fetched ${f.fetchedAt}${f.own ? '' : ' (page asOf)'}${f.stale ? ' STALE' : ''}` : 'no fetch time'}${recipe}${edited}${run}${change}${mark}`
   })
   if (fresh.length) lines.push(`Freshness: ${fresh.join('; ')}.`)
   for (const block of dashboard.blocks) {
     if (block.kind === 'kpi') {
       const r = kpiResult(dashboard, block, state)
       const rule = firstRule(block.rules, r.value)
       lines.push(
-        `kpi ${block.id} = ${formatValue(r.value, block.format, dashboard.currency)}${r.value === null ? '' : ` (raw ${Number(r.value.toPrecision(12))})`}${r.delta ? `, ${r.delta.text} ${r.delta.label}` : ''}${rule ? `, flagged ${ruleWord(rule)}` : ''}${r.period !== null ? `, period ${r.period}` : ''}, ${r.rowCount} rows`,
+        `kpi ${block.id} = ${formatValue(r.value, block.format, dashboard.currency, block.percentDigits)}${r.value === null ?        )
     } else if (block.kind === 'chart') {
       const data = chartData(dashboard, block, state)
       const series = data.series
         .filter((s) => !s.trend)
         .slice(0, 4)
         .map((s) => {
           const format = s.previous ? chartValueFormat(dashboard, block) : seriesValueFormat(dashboard, block, s.key)
           const show = (v: number | null) => formatValue(v, format, dashboard.currency)
           const additive = (block.agg === undefined || block.agg === 'sum' || block.agg === 'count') && format !== 'percent'
           return seriesLine(data, s.key, s.label, show, additive)
         })
       const facets = block.facet ? `, ${chartFacets(dashboard, block, state).length} small multiples` : ''
       lines.push(`chart ${block.id} (${block.type}) ${data.rows.length} points${facets}: ${series.join(' | ')}`)
     } else if (block.kind === 'table') {
       lines.push(`table ${block.id}: ${datasetRows(dashboard, block.datasetId, state).length} rows`)
     }
   }
   const text = lines.join('\n')
   return text.length > limit ? `${text.slice(0, limit - 1)}…` : text
 }

 export type DashboardParse =
   | { ok: true; dashboard: Dashboard }
   | { ok: false; issues: string[] }
 
 /** Validate an untrusted dashboard body. Never throws; issues read as `path: message`. */
 export function parseDashboard(input: unknown): DashboardParse {
   const result = dashboardSchema.safeParse(input)
   if (result.success) return { ok: true, dashboard: result.data }
   return {
     ok: false,
     issues: result.error.issues.map((issue) => {
       const path = issue.path.map(String).join('.')
       return path ? `${path}: ${issue.message}` : issue.message
     }),
   }
 }

 Column types: string | number | date (ISO "YYYY-MM-DD") | currency (a number in major units) | percent (a FRACTION: 0.125 = 12.5%). A cell may be null; row keys must be declared columns; ids are letters, digits, "-" or "_".
 
 Blocks (each has a unique "id" and a "kind"):
 - kpi: { "kind":"kpi", "id", "label", "datasetId"?, "value": {"column","agg"} | {"literal": 1234} | {"ratio": {"numerator": {column,agg}, "denominator": {column,agg}}},

 - chart: { "kind":"chart", "id", "title", "type": bar | line | area | stacked-bar | pie | scatter | combo | horizontal-bar | donut | funnel, "datasetId", "x": column, "series": [{ "key": numeric column, "label", "as"?: "bar" | "line" (combo only) }] (1-${MAX_SERIES}), "sourceId",

 A kpi or chart with a datasetId must use that dataset's sourceId.
 Filter: { "id", "label", "datasetIds": [...], "column", "type": "select" | "date-range", "default"? }; the column exists in every listed dataset; date-range needs a date column.  /**
  * Code export: one board's node tree as readable source a developer (or a coding agent) can
  * paste into a project. Two targets from one walk:
  *   - `boardToReact`: a React component styled with Tailwind utility classes (arbitrary values
  *     where the scale has no exact match), icons from lucide-react.
  *   - `boardToHtml`: a standalone HTML document with one CSS class per node and the design's
  *     colour tokens as CSS custom properties.
  * Instances are expanded (the main's content with the instance's overrides), hidden nodes are
  * left out, and the layout mirrors the renderer's: stacks are flexbox, grids CSS grid, absolute
  * frames positioned children. Pure and deterministic (no DOM), so it runs on the server too.
  */
 
 // ---- the shared description: an element tree with CSS declarations
 
 type Decl = [property: string, value: string]
 type El = {
   tag: string
   /** A readable class name base (from the layer name or the node's id). */
   name: string
   attrs: Array<[string, string]>
   css: Decl[]
   children: Array<El | string>
   /** React only: a lucide icon component in place of the element. */
@@ -150,158 +150,168 @@ function hasText(node: DesignNode, mains: ReadonlyMap<string, FrameNode>, depth
   if (depth > 40) return false
   if (node.type === 'text') return node.text.trim().length > 0
   if (node.type === 'button') return true
   if (node.type === 'image') return node.alt.trim().length > 0
   if (node.type !== 'frame') return false
   const shown = node.instanceOf !== undefined ? resolveInstance(node, mains) : node
   return shown.children.some((child) => !child.hidden && hasText(child, mains, depth + 1))
 }
 
 /** A colour as CSS: a token becomes var(--color-name) (HTML) or its hex (Tailwind). */
 function color(value: string | undefined, ctx: Ctx): string | undefined {
   if (value === undefined) return undefined
   if (value === 'transparent' || HEX.test(value)) return value
   const hex = ctx.tokens?.colors[value] ?? DEFAULT_COLORS[value]
   if (!hex) return undefined
   return ctx.useVars ? `var(--color-${value})` : hex
 }
 
 const fontStackOf = (tokens: DesignTokens | undefined, role: 'heading' | 'body' | 'mono') =>
   role === 'mono' ? FONT_STACKS.mono.stack : FONT_STACKS[tokens?.fonts?.[role] ?? 'system'].stack
 /** A font role: var(--font-heading) etc. in HTML, the stack itself for Tailwind. */
 const font = (ctx: Ctx, role: 'heading' | 'body' | 'mono') => (ctx.useVars ? `var(--font-${role})` : fontStackOf(ctx.tokens, role))
 
 const pxv = (value: number) => `${Math.round(value * 100) / 100}px`

+function spacingCss(spacing: Padding | undefined): string | undefined {
+  if (spacing === undefined) return undefined
+  if (typeof spacing === 'number') return pxv(spacing)
+  return spacing.map(pxv).join(' ')

+function fillInsideMargin(margin: Padding | undefined, axis: 'width' | 'height', base = '100%'): string {
+  if (margin === undefined) return base
+  const [top, right, bottom = top, left = right] = typeof margin === 'number' ? [margin, margin] : margin
+  const total = axis === 'width' ? left + right : top + bottom
+  return total === 0 ? base : `calc(${base} - ${pxv(total)})`
 }

   w?: Size; h?: Size; grow?: boolean; x?: number; y?: number
   opacity?: number; absolute?: boolean; anchor?: string; aspect?: number; blur?: number
+  margin?: Padding

 /** How a node sizes and places itself in its parent: the renderer's rules, as CSS. */
 function sizeCss(node: Sized, parent: Parent, defaults: { w?: Size; h?: Size } = {}): Decl[] {
   const out: Decl[] = []
   const w = node.w ?? defaults.w ?? 'hug'
   const h = node.h ?? defaults.h ?? 'hug'
   if (node.absolute && parent !== 'absolute' && parent !== 'root') {
     const anchor = node.anchor ?? 'top-left'
     out.push(['position', 'absolute'])
     out.push([anchor.startsWith('bottom') ? 'bottom' : 'top', pxv(node.y ?? 0)])
     out.push([anchor.endsWith('right') ? 'right' : 'left', pxv(node.x ?? 0)])
     if (typeof w === 'number') out.push(['width', pxv(w)])
     if (typeof h === 'number') out.push(['height', pxv(h)])
   } else if (parent === 'root') {
-    out.push(['width', typeof w === 'number' ? pxv(w) : '100%'])
+    out.push(['width', typeof w === 'number' ? pxv(w) : fillInsideMargin(node.margin, 'width')])
     if (typeof h === 'number') out.push(['height', pxv(h)])
-    else out.push(['min-height', '100%'])
+    else out.push(['min-height', fillInsideMargin(node.margin, 'height')])
   } else if (parent === 'absolute') {
     out.push(['position', 'absolute'], ['left', pxv(node.x ?? 0)], ['top', pxv(node.y ?? 0)])
     if (typeof w === 'number') out.push(['width', pxv(w)])
     else if (w === 'fill') out.push(['right', '0'])
     if (typeof h === 'number') out.push(['height', pxv(h)])
     else if (h === 'fill') out.push(['bottom', '0'])
   } else if (parent === 'grid') {
     if (typeof w === 'number') out.push(['width', pxv(w)])
     if (typeof h === 'number') out.push(['height', pxv(h)])
-    else if (h === 'fill') out.push(['height', '100%'])
+    else if (h === 'fill') out.push(['height', fillInsideMargin(node.margin, 'height')])
   } else {
     const row = parent === 'stack-h'
     const main = row ? w : h
     const cross = row ? h : w
     if (main === 'fill' || node.grow) out.push(['flex', '1 1 0%'], [row ? 'min-width' : 'min-height', '0'])
     else if (typeof main === 'number') out.push([row ? 'width' : 'height', pxv(main)], ['flex-shrink', '0'])
     if (cross === 'fill') out.push(['align-self', 'stretch'])
     else if (typeof cross === 'number') out.push([row ? 'height' : 'width', pxv(cross)])
   }
+  const margin = spacingCss(node.margin)
+  if (margin !== undefined) out.push(['margin', margin])
   if (node.opacity !== undefined && node.opacity < 1) out.push(['opacity', String(node.opacity)])
   if (node.aspect) out.push(['aspect-ratio', String(Math.round(node.aspect * 10000) / 10000)])
   if (node.blur) out.push(['filter', `blur(${node.blur}px)`])
   return out
 }
 
 const gradientCss = (g: { from: string; to: string; angle?: number; kind?: 'linear' | 'radial' }, ctx: Ctx) =>
   g.kind === 'radial'
     ? `radial-gradient(circle at center, ${color(g.from, ctx)}, ${color(g.to, ctx)})`
     : `linear-gradient(${g.angle ?? 180}deg, ${color(g.from, ctx)}, ${color(g.to, ctx)})`
 
 function nameOf(node: DesignNode): string {
   const base = (node.name ?? node.id.split('~').pop() ?? node.type)
     .toLowerCase()
     .replace(/[^a-z0-9]+/g, '-')
     .replace(/^-+|-+$/g, '')
   return base && /^[a-z]/.test(base) ? base : `${node.type}-${base || 'node'}`
 }
 
 function frameEl(raw: FrameNode, parent: Parent, ctx: Ctx, depth: number): El {

     // Children that are positioned (absolute layout or pinned) need a containing block.

   }
   if (shown.layout === 'stack-v' || shown.layout === 'stack-h') {
     css.push(['display', 'flex'], ['flex-direction', shown.layout === 'stack-h' ? 'row' : 'column'])
     const align = shown.align ?? (shown.layout === 'stack-h' ? 'center' : 'start')
     if (align !== 'stretch') css.push(['align-items', ALIGN[align]])
     if (shown.justify && shown.justify !== 'start') css.push(['justify-content', JUSTIFY[shown.justify]])
     if (shown.wrap) css.push(['flex-wrap', 'wrap'])
     if (shown.gap) css.push(['gap', pxv(shown.gap)])
     if (shown.wrap && shown.rowGap !== undefined) css.push(['row-gap', pxv(shown.rowGap)])

 function nodeEl(raw: DesignNode, parent: Parent, ctx: Ctx, depth: number): El {
   const el = nodeElInner(resolveNode(raw, { styles: ctx.styles }), parent, ctx, depth)
   if (raw.type === 'frame') return el
   const small = mergeSmall(el.small, narrowDecls(raw, ctx))
   return small ? { ...el, small } : el
 }
 
 /** A node's override at the board's narrow breakpoint, as the CSS that replaces its own there. */
 function narrowDecls(node: DesignNode, ctx: Ctx): Decl[] | undefined {
   const override: ResponsiveOverride | undefined = ctx.narrow ? node.responsive?.[ctx.narrow] : undefined
   if (!override) return undefined
   const out: Decl[] = []
   if (override.hidden === true) out.push(['display', 'none'])
   if (node.type === 'frame') {
     if (override.layout === 'stack-v' || override.layout === 'stack-h') out.push(['flex-direction', override.layout === 'stack-h' ? 'row' : 'column'])
     if (override.gap !== undefined) out.push(['gap', pxv(override.gap)])
-    const padding = paddingCss(override.padding)
+    const padding = spacingCss(override.padding)
     if (padding) out.push(['padding', padding])
     if (override.columns !== undefined) out.push(['grid-template-columns', override.columns === 1 ? 'minmax(0, 1fr)' : `repeat(${override.columns}, minmax(0, 1fr))`])
   }
   if (node.type === 'text' && (override.fontSize !== undefined || override.style !== undefined)) {
     out.push(['font-size', pxv(override.fontSize ?? TEXT_SCALE[override.style ?? node.style].size)])
   }
   if (node.type === 'text' && override.weight !== undefined) out.push(['font-weight', String(override.weight)])
-  if (override.w !== undefined) out.push(['width', typeof override.w === 'number' ? pxv(override.w) : override.w === 'fill' ? '100%' : 'auto'])
+  if (override.w !== undefined) out.push(['width', typeof override.w === 'number' ? pxv(override.w) : override.w === 'fill' ? fillInsideMargin(node.margin, 'width') : 'auto'])
   return out.length ? out : undefined
 }
 
 function mergeSmall(a: Decl[] | undefined, b: Decl[] | undefined): Decl[] | undefined {
   if (!a?.length) return b
   if (!b?.length) return a
   const props = new Set(b.map(([prop]) => prop))
   return [...a.filter(([prop]) => !props.has(prop)), ...b]
 }

 /** A vector shape as an inline SVG: one path, filled when closed, stroked otherwise. */
 function vectorEl(node: ShapeNode, parent: Parent, ctx: Ctx): El {

   const h = typeof node.h === 'number' ? node.h : 100
   const geometry = shapeGeometry(node, w, h)
   const stroke = color(node.stroke, ctx) ?? (geometry.closed ? undefined : color('text', ctx))
   const fill = geometry.closed ? color(node.fill, ctx) ?? (stroke ? undefined : color('primary', ctx)) : undefined

+    const dashes = strokeDashArray(node.strokePattern, width)
+    if (dashes) path.push(['stroke-dasharray', dashes], ['vector-effect', 'non-scaling-stroke'])
+  }
   return {
     tag: 'svg',
     name: nameOf(node),
     attrs: [['viewBox', `0 0 ${w} ${h}`], ['preserveAspectRatio', 'none'], ['aria-hidden', 'true']],
     css: [...sizeCss(node, parent), ['display', 'block'], ['overflow', 'visible']],
     children: [{ tag: 'path', name: `${nameOf(node)}-path`, attrs: [['d', geometry.d]], css: path, children: [] }],
   }

 // ---- helpers shared by both targets
 
 const escapeText = (value: string) => value.replace(/&/g, '&amp;').replace(/</g, '&lt;').replace(/>/g, '&gt;')
 const escapeAttr = (value: string) => escapeText(value).replace(/"/g, '&quot;')
 /**
  * A value inside a double-quoted CSS string (`url("…")`): every character that could end the
  * string, the url() or the `<style>` element (quotes, backslash, parens, `<`, `>`, line breaks)
  * as a CSS hex escape, so a stored src can never break out of the stylesheet.
  */
 function cssString(value: string): string {
   return value.replace(/[\\"'()<>\u0000-\u001f\u007f\u2028\u2029]/g, (ch) => `\\${ch.charCodeAt(0).toString(16)} `)
 }
 /** Text inside a `/* … *\/` comment: it cannot close the comment or break the line. */
 const commentText = (value: string) => value.replace(/[\r\n\u2028\u2029]+/g, ' ').replace(/\*\//g, '* /')

+/** Use the widest authored layout that fits, or the narrowest when all are wider. */
+export function breakpointAtWidth(board: DesignBoard, width: number): string | null {
+  const widths = [{ id: null, w: board.w }, ...(board.breakpoints ?? [])]
+  const fitting = widths.filter((entry) => entry.w <= width).sort((a, b) => b.w - a.w)
+  return (fitting[0] ?? widths.sort((a, b) => a.w - b.w)[0]).id

 /** Every breakpoint id any board declares, in first-seen order (the editor's switcher). */
 export function designBreakpoints(design: Design): Breakpoint[] {
   const seen = new Map<string, Breakpoint>()
   for (const board of design.boards) for (const entry of board.breakpoints ?? []) if (!seen.has(entry.id)) seen.set(entry.id, entry)
   return [...seen.values()]
 }
 
 /** The node as drawn at `breakpointId`: its override's keys (those its type honours) win. */
 export function applyResponsive<T extends DesignNode>(node: T, breakpointId: string | null | undefined): T {
   if (!breakpointId || !node.responsive) return node
   const override = node.responsive[breakpointId]
   if (!override) return node
   const out: Record<string, unknown> = { ...node }
   for (const key of KEYS_BY_TYPE[node.type]) {
     const value = override[key]
     if (value !== undefined) out[key] = value
   }
   return out as T
 }

 // ---- editing at a breakpoint
 
 /**
  * Splits a node patch for the active breakpoint: keys the type honours there go to the override,
  * the rest (text, colours, names) to the base node. With no breakpoint everything is base.
  */
 export function routePatch(
   type: NodeType,
   patch: Record<string, unknown>,
   breakpointId: string | null | undefined,
 ): { base: Record<string, unknown>; override: Record<string, unknown> } {
   if (!breakpointId) return { base: patch, override: {} }
   const keys = new Set<string>(KEYS_BY_TYPE[type])
   const base: Record<string, unknown> = {}
   const override: Record<string, unknown> = {}
   for (const [key, value] of Object.entries(patch)) (keys.has(key) ? override : base)[key] = value
   return { base, override }
 }

 /** Regular polygon points inscribed in the box (first vertex at the top). */
 export function polygonPoints(sides: number, w: number, h: number): Array<[number, number]> {
   const n = Math.max(3, Math.min(24, Math.round(sides)))
   const out: Array<[number, number]> = []
   for (let i = 0; i < n; i += 1) {
     const angle = -Math.PI / 2 + (i * 2 * Math.PI) / n
     out.push([r(w / 2 + (w / 2) * Math.cos(angle)), r(h / 2 + (h / 2) * Math.sin(angle))])
   }
   return out
 }

 /**
  * An https URL, or a same-origin path with exactly one leading slash. https stays valid so stored
  * designs keep parsing; the agent is told never to hotlink (import_artifact_image gives an
  * `/_asset/<id>` ref), the tool flags one, and the views draw it as a placeholder (remote-images.ts).
  */
 export function isSafeImageSrc(src: string): boolean {
   if (src.length > 2048 || /[\s\\]/.test(src) || /[\u0000-\u001f]/.test(src)) return false
   if (src.startsWith('/')) return isSameOriginImagePath(src)
   try {
     const url = new URL(src)
     return url.protocol === 'https:' && url.username === '' && url.password === ''
   } catch {
     return false
   }
 }

 /** Delete / Backspace: clear values and formulas in a rect, keeping the formatting. */
 export function clearRange(workbook: Workbook, tabId: string, rect: CellRect): Workbook {
   const tab = tabById(workbook, tabId)
   if (!tab) return workbook
   const updates = new Map<string, SheetCell | undefined>()
   for (const address of filledIn(tab, rect)) {
     const { v: _v, f: _f, ...style } = tab.cells[address]
     updates.set(address, style)
   }
   return setCells(workbook, tabId, updates)
 }

 const BOOLEAN_STYLES = ['bold', 'italic', 'underline', 'strike', 'wrap'] as const
 const VALUE_STYLES = ['fmt', 'align', 'bg', 'color', 'size', 'valign', 'decimals', 'dateStyle', 'note', 'font', 'indent', 'rotate', 'list', 'pattern'] as const
 
 /** Formatting over a rect. `null` removes that property. Capped so a whole-sheet select cannot explode. */
 export function formatRange(workbook: Workbook, tabId: string, rect: CellRect, patch: FormatPatch): Workbook {
   const tab = tabById(workbook, tabId)
   if (!tab) return workbook
   const size = rectSize(rect)
   const updates = new Map<string, SheetCell | undefined>()
   const apply = (address: string, pos: CellPos) => {
     const old = tab.cells[address]
     let cell: SheetCell = { ...(old ?? {}) }
     if (patch.clear)
       cell = {
         ...(old?.v !== undefined ? { v: old.v } : {}),
         ...(old?.f !== undefined ? { f: old.f } : {}),
         // A person cell's people and a list's items are content, not formatting.
         ...(old?.people ? { people: old.people } : {}),
         ...(old?.list ? { list: old.list } : {}),
       }
     const record = cell as Record<string, unknown>
     for (const key of BOOLEAN_STYLES) {
       if (patch[key] === undefined) continue
       if (patch[key]) record[key] = true
       else delete record[key]
     }
     for (const key of VALUE_STYLES) {
       const value = patch[key]
       if (value === undefined) continue
       if (value === null || value === '') delete record[key]
       else record[key] = value
     }
     if (patch.border) {
-      const style = patch.border.style ?? 'thin'
+      const { style = 'thin', color, preset } = patch.border
+      const line = color ? { style, color } : style
       const border: NonNullable<SheetCell['border']> = { ...(cell.border ?? {}) }
-      const preset = patch.border.preset
       if (preset === 'none') {
         delete cell.border
       } else {
         const edge = {
           top: pos.row === rect.top,
           bottom: pos.row === rect.bottom,
           left: pos.col === rect.left,
           right: pos.col === rect.right,
         }
         for (const side of ['top', 'right', 'bottom', 'left'] as const) {
-          if (preset === 'all' || (preset === 'outer' && edge[side]) || (preset === side && edge[side])) border[side] = style
+          if (preset === 'all' || (preset === 'outer' && edge[side]) || (preset === side && edge[side])) border[side] = line
         }
+        if ((preset === 'inner' || preset === 'horizontal') && !edge.bottom) border.bottom = line
+        if ((preset === 'inner' || preset === 'vertical') && !edge.right) border.right = line
         cell.border = border
       }
     }
     updates.set(address, cell)
   }
   // Removing formatting only touches filled cells; adding it to a huge rect stops at the cell cap.
   const removing =
     Boolean(patch.clear) ||
     patch.border?.preset === 'none' ||
     BOOLEAN_STYLES.some((key) => patch[key] === false) ||
     VALUE_STYLES.some((key) => patch[key] === null)
   if (removing || size.rows * size.cols > 20_000) {
     if (!removing) {
       // Format the filled cells plus the first screenful, so a whole-column select still shows it.
       forEachCell({ ...rect, bottom: Math.min(rect.bottom, rect.top + Math.floor(20_000 / size.cols) - 1) }, apply)
     }
     for (const address of filledIn(tab, rect)) apply(address, parseAddressFast(address))
   } else forEachCell(rect, apply)
   return setCells(workbook, tabId, updates)
 }
 
 function parseAddressFast(address: string): CellPos {
   const match = /^([A-Z]+)(\d+)$/.exec(address)!
   let col = 0
   for (const ch of match[1]) col = col * 26 + (ch.charCodeAt(0) - 64)

 /** Whether `zone` is an IANA time zone this runtime knows ("Europe/London", "UTC"). */
 export function isTimeZone(zone: string): boolean {
   if (!/^[A-Za-z][A-Za-z0-9_+\-/]{0,63}$/.test(zone)) return false
   try {
     new Intl.DateTimeFormat('en-US', { timeZone: zone })
     return true
   } catch {
     return false
   }
 }

   Math.max(min, Math.min(max, value))
 
 /** A fresh id not used in the deck: `<base>-2`, `<base>-3`, … (or `slide-N`). Deterministic. */
 export function uniqueSlideId(deck: Deck, base = 'slide'): string {
   const used = new Set(deck.slides.map((slide) => slide.id))
   const stem = (base.replace(/-\d+$/, '') || 'slide').slice(0, LIMITS.idLength - 4)
   for (let n = 2; ; n++) {
     const candidate = `${stem}-${n}`
     if (!used.has(candidate)) return candidate
   }
 }

 /** Copy the slide with `id` to just after it, under a fresh id. */
 export function duplicateSlide(deck: Deck, id: string): { deck: Deck; id: string } {
   const index = deck.slides.findIndex((slide) => slide.id === id)
   if (index === -1 || deck.slides.length >= LIMITS.slides) return { deck, id }
   const copyId = uniqueSlideId(deck, id)
   const copy: Slide = structuredClone({ ...deck.slides[index], id: copyId })
   // A copy continues its original's section; it does not start another one of the same name.
   delete copy.section
   delete copy.sectionDescription
   const slides = [...deck.slides]
   slides.splice(index + 1, 0, copy)
   return { deck: { ...deck, slides }, id: copyId }
 }
 
 /**
  * Remove the slide with `id`. The last slide cannot be removed. Returns the id that should be
  * selected next (the slide that took its place, or the new last one).
  */
 export function deleteSlide(deck: Deck, id: string): { deck: Deck; selectId: string } {
   const index = deck.slides.findIndex((slide) => slide.id === id)
   if (index === -1 || deck.slides.length <= 1) return { deck, selectId: id }
   const slides = deck.slides.filter((slide) => slide.id !== id)
-  return { deck: { ...deck, slides }, selectId: slides[Math.min(index, slides.length - 1)].id }
+  return { deck: afterSlideRemoval(deck, slides), selectId: slides[Math.min(index, slides.length - 1)].id }
 }
 
 /** Move the slide at `from` so it lands at index `to` (both clamped). */
 export function moveSlide(deck: Deck, from: number, to: number): Deck {
   const last = deck.slides.length - 1
   if (from < 0 || from > last) return deck
   const target = clamp(to, 0, last)
   if (target === from) return deck
   const slides = [...deck.slides]
   const [moved] = slides.splice(from, 1)
   slides.splice(target, 0, moved)
   return { ...deck, slides }
 }
 
 /**

  * exactly once; otherwise the deck comes back unchanged and `ok` is false.
  */

   const byId = new Map(deck.slides.map((slide) => [slide.id, slide]))
   if (ids.length !== deck.slides.length || new Set(ids).size !== ids.length) {
     return { deck, ok: false }
   }
   const slides: Slide[] = []
   for (const id of ids) {
@@ -276,75 +277,107 @@ export function setSlideSection(deck: Deck, id: string, name: string): Deck {
 export function setSectionDescription(deck: Deck, id: string, text: string): Deck {
   const index = deck.slides.findIndex((slide) => slide.id === id)
   if (index === -1) return deck
   const current = deck.slides[index]
   const clipped = text.replace(/[ \t]+/g, ' ').trim().slice(0, LIMITS.sectionDescription)
   if (!current.section && clipped) return deck
   if ((current.sectionDescription ?? '') === clipped) return deck
   const next: Slide = { ...current }
   if (clipped) next.sectionDescription = clipped
   else delete next.sectionDescription
   const slides = [...deck.slides]
   slides[index] = next
   return { ...deck, slides }
 }

 /** How present mode arrives at the slide at `index`: its own, else the deck's, else fade. */
 export function slideTransition(deck: Pick<Deck, 'transition' | 'slides'>, index: number): DeckTransition {
   return deck.slides[index]?.transition ?? deck.transition ?? 'fade'
 }

 // ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
 // Several slides at once (the filmstrip's multi-select).
 
 /** Remove the slides with these ids; at least one slide always stays (the first not removed, else the first). */
 export function deleteSlides(deck: Deck, ids: readonly string[]): { deck: Deck; selectId: string } {
   const drop = new Set(ids)
   const first = deck.slides.findIndex((slide) => drop.has(slide.id))
   if (first === -1) return { deck, selectId: deck.slides[0].id }
   let slides = deck.slides.filter((slide) => !drop.has(slide.id))
   // Never empty: keep the first selected slide.
   if (!slides.length) slides = [deck.slides[first]]
   if (slides.length === deck.slides.length) return { deck, selectId: deck.slides[first].id }
-  return { deck: { ...deck, slides }, selectId: slides[Math.min(first, slides.length - 1)].id }
+  return { deck: afterSlideRemoval(deck, slides), selectId: slides[Math.min(first, slides.length - 1)].id }
 }
 
 /** Copy the slides with these ids, as one block after the last of them, in deck order. */
 export function duplicateSlides(deck: Deck, ids: readonly string[]): { deck: Deck; ids: string[] } {
   const chosen = deck.slides.filter((slide) => ids.includes(slide.id))
   if (!chosen.length || deck.slides.length + chosen.length > LIMITS.slides) return { deck, ids: [] }
   let working = deck
   const copies: Slide[] = []
   for (const slide of chosen) {
     const copyId = uniqueSlideId(working, slide.id)
     const copy: Slide = structuredClone({ ...slide, id: copyId })
     delete copy.section
     delete copy.sectionDescription
     copies.push(copy)
     working = { ...working, slides: [...working.slides, copy] }
   }
   const last = Math.max(...chosen.map((slide) => deck.slides.indexOf(slide)))
   const slides = [...deck.slides]
   slides.splice(last + 1, 0, ...copies)
   return { deck: { ...deck, slides }, ids: copies.map((copy) => copy.id) }
 }

 // Aspect ratio
 
 /**
  * Switch the deck to `aspect` and reflow its free elements to the new canvas width: each is
  * scaled by the width ratio about the vertical line it sat on (a 16:9 box a quarter of the way
  * across stays a quarter of the way across) and kept on the same vertical centre; text sizes
  * scale with it. The layouts reflow by themselves. Undoing restores the exact old deck.
  */
 export function setDeckAspect(deck: Deck, aspect: DeckAspect): Deck {
   if (deck.aspect === aspect) return deck
   const factor = DESIGN_SIZE[aspect].width / DESIGN_SIZE[deck.aspect].width
   const slides = deck.slides.map((slide) => {
     if (!slide.elements?.length) return slide
     return { ...slide, elements: slide.elements.map((element) => reflowElement(element, factor)) }
   })
   return { ...deck, aspect, slides }
 }
 
 function reflowElement(element: SlideElement, factor: number): SlideElement {
   const w = element.w * factor
   const h = element.type === 'line' || element.type === 'arrow' || element.type === 'connector' ? element.h : element.h * factor
   const cy = element.y + element.h / 2
   const box = clampBox({ x: element.x * factor, y: cy - h / 2, w, h })
   const next = { ...element, ...box } as SlideElement & { size?: number }
   if ('size' in element && typeof element.size === 'number')
     next.size = round(clamp(element.size * factor, ELEMENT_BOUNDS.minFont, ELEMENT_BOUNDS.maxFont))
+  if (next.type === 'rect' && next.radius !== undefined) {
+    next.radius = round(Math.min(next.radius * factor, ELEMENT_BOUNDS.maxSize / 2))
+  }
   return next
 }

 // Design-system tokens
 
 export type DeckTokens = NonNullable<Deck['tokens']>
 
 const HEX6 = /^#[0-9a-fA-F]{6}$/
 const HEX3 = /^#[0-9a-fA-F]{3}$/
 
 /** A colour as #rrggbb (from #rgb, #rrggbb or #rrggbbaa), or null. */
 export function normaliseHex(value: unknown): string | null {
   if (typeof value !== 'string') return null
   const text = value.trim()
   if (HEX6.test(text)) return text.toLowerCase()
   if (HEX3.test(text)) return `#${[...text.slice(1)].map((c) => c + c).join('')}`.toLowerCase()
   if (/^#[0-9a-fA-F]{8}$/.test(text)) return text.slice(0, 7).toLowerCase()
   return null
 }
@@ -554,52 +557,52 @@ function luminance(hex: string): number {
   }
   return 0.2126 * channel(1) + 0.7152 * channel(3) + 0.0722 * channel(5)
 }
 
 /** The WCAG contrast ratio of two #rrggbb colours (1 to 21), rounded to a hundredth. */
 export function contrastRatio(a: string, b: string): number {
   const [hi, lo] = [luminance(a), luminance(b)].sort((x, y) => y - x)
   return Math.round(((hi + 0.05) / (lo + 0.05)) * 100) / 100
 }
 
 /** `a` mixed into `b` at `share` (0-1), as #rrggbb (CSS color-mix in srgb). */
 export function mixHex(a: string, b: string, share: number): string {
   const part = (hex: string, offset: number) => parseInt(hex.slice(offset, offset + 2), 16)
   const out = [1, 3, 5].map((offset) => Math.round(part(a, offset) * share + part(b, offset) * (1 - share)))
   return `#${out.map((value) => value.toString(16).padStart(2, '0')).join('')}`
 }
 
 /**
  * A deck's colour roles as #rrggbb for one app palette: the deck's tokens where it has them, else
  * the app's light or dark theme (approximately: the slide CSS draws app tokens, which this file
  * cannot read). `tint` is the accent at 16% over the paper, as the canvas mixes it.
  */

     mode === 'light'
-      ? { ink: '#1a1a1d', soft: '#5c5c66', accent: '#507e35', paper: '#ffffff' }
-      : { ink: '#ebebeb', soft: '#9e9e9e', accent: '#a7d78e', paper: '#171717' }
+      ? { ink: '#1a1a1d', soft: '#5c5c66', accent: '#5e774e', paper: '#ffffff' }
+      : { ink: '#ebebeb', soft: '#9e9e9e', accent: '#b5cea5', paper: '#171717' }
   const colors = deck.tokens?.colors ?? {}
   const ink = colors.ink ?? app.ink
   const soft = colors.soft ?? app.soft
   const accent = colors.accent ?? app.accent
   const paper = colors.paper ?? app.paper
   return { ink, soft, accent, paper, tint: colors.tint ?? mixHex(accent, paper, 0.16) }
 }

 /**
  * Freebuff Slides — the deck content model (version 1).
  *
  * ===========================================================================
  * FOR AN AGENT AUTHORING A DECK
  * ===========================================================================
  * A deck is ONE JSON object. It is declarative data, never HTML: there is no
  * field that takes markup, CSS or script, and Markdown bodies are rendered
  * without raw HTML (any `<tag>` shows as text). Unknown keys are rejected, so
  * a typo fails validation instead of being silently dropped.
  *
  *   {
  *     "version": 1,
  *     "title": "Q4 launch plan",            // 1-200 chars
  *     "aspect": "16:9",                      // or "4:3"
  *     "theme": "plain",                      // optional: "plain" | "tinted" | "bold"
  *     "accent": "brand",                     // optional: "brand" | "blue" | "green" | "amber" | "red"
  *     "transition": "fade",                  // optional, present mode: "none" | "fade" | "slide" | "push" | "magic" (absent: fade)
  *     "fonts": { "heading": "serif", "body": "system" },  // optional, DECK_FONT_IDS (system stacks + the app's web fonts)
  *     "tokens": { "name": "Acme", "colors": { "accent": "#0057ff" }, "spacing": "roomy" },  // optional design system
  *     "footer": "Acme · Confidential",       // optional, <= 120 chars; absent: the deck title, "": none
  *     "slideNumbers": false,                 // optional; absent: numbers shown
+ *     "thumbnailSlideId": "cover",          // optional: which slide appears on the deck card
  *     "slides": [ Slide, ... ]               // 1-200 slides, in presenting order
  *   }
  *
  * Slide:
  *   {
  *     "id": "cover",            // unique in the deck; letters, digits, "-" or "_", 1-64 chars.
  *                               // Keep ids stable: edits, comments and "Ask Buffy" address slides by id.
  *     "layout": "title",        // see the table below
  *     "title": "...",           // optional, <= 200 chars, plain text
  *     "subtitle": "...",        // optional, <= 300 chars, plain text
  *     "body": "...",            // optional Markdown, <= 4000 chars
  *     "columns": ["md", "md"],  // exactly two Markdown strings, each <= 4000 chars
  *     "image": { "src": "/_asset/<id>", "alt": "...", "fit": "contain" },  // fit optional: "contain" | "cover"
  *                               // position optional: { "x": 0-100, "y": 0-100 }, the point kept in view under cover
  *     "section": "Results",     // optional, <= 80 chars: this slide starts a section of that name
  *     "chart": { ...the Docs chart spec, see below... },
  *     "notes": "...",           // optional speaker notes, plain text, <= 4000 chars
  *     "style": { "title": { "size": "lg", "align": "center", "color": "accent" } },
  *                               // optional per element (title | subtitle | body): size sm|md|lg|xl,
  *                               // align start|center|end, color ink|soft|accent (theme roles)
  *     "build": "bullets",       // optional: present mode reveals top-level bullets one per click ("all": nested too)
  *     "sectionDescription": "…", // optional, <= 300 chars, with `section`: what that section covers
  *     "transition": "slide",    // optional: how present mode arrives at this slide (absent: the deck's)
+ *     "background": "#222423", // optional: this slide's canvas colour (absent: deck theme)
+ *     "skipped": true,         // optional: skip during playback
  *     "elements": [ Element ]   // optional, <= 40: free-position text boxes, shapes, lines, arrows,
  *                               // images above the layout, back to front (see SLIDES_GUIDE)
  *   }
  *
  * Layouts (the canvas is a fixed 960x540 for 16:9, 720x540 for 4:3, scaled to fit):
  *   title       Cover. Big `title`, `subtitle` under it. `body` is ignored.
  *   section     Divider between parts. `title`, optional `subtitle`.
  *   title-body  `title` on top, `body` below: bullets, numbered lists, bold, links, tables.
  *   two-column  `title`, then `columns` side by side. REQUIRES `columns`.
  *   image       `title` (optional), one picture, `subtitle` as caption. REQUIRES `image`.
  *   chart       `title`, one chart, `body` as a one-line takeaway. REQUIRES `chart`.
  *   quote       `body` is the quotation (Markdown), `subtitle` the attribution.
  *   blank       Only `body`, centred. Use sparingly.
  * Text fields are always optional (a person may clear one while editing); only
  * the payload a layout cannot draw without (columns, image, chart) is required.
  *
  * Markdown (GitHub-flavoured): `- bullets`, `1. numbered`, `**bold**`, `*italic*`,
  * `[links](https://...)`, `` `code` ``, pipe tables. Keep a slide to ~6 bullets or
  * one table of <= 6 rows; long text does not shrink to fit, it overflows.
  *
  * image.src: an uploaded `/_asset/<id>` ref: for a picture from the web, call
  * `import_artifact_image` first and use the ref it returns (read_artifact's `assets` lists the
  * deck's). Never hotlink. The schema still accepts an `https://` URL so stored decks keep
  * validating, but the tool flags one and every view draws it as a placeholder (the workspace
  * until the reader clicks "Load images", a share link always; remote-images.ts). No `data:`,
  * `http:` or `javascript:` URLs.
  *
  * chart: the same spec as a Docs ```chart block, as an object (not a string):
  *   { "type": "bar" | "line" | "area", "title"?: string, "x": "<row key for the x axis>",
  *     "series": [{ "key": "<row key>", "label": "<legend label>" }],   // 1-8, unique, not x
  *     "data": [{ "<x>": "Week 1", "<series key>": 40 }, ...] }           // 1-500 rows
- *   Every row has the x key; series values are finite numbers. Exactly the
- *   rules of `parseChartSpec` in doc/DocChart.tsx (a test keeps them in step).
+ *   Every row has the x key; series values are finite numbers. Docs and Slides share chartSpecSchema.
  *
  * Editing: rewrite one slide by id (`replaceSlide`), reorder with the full id
  * list (`reorderSlides`), add or remove one at a time (`slides-ops.ts`). Always
  * send the whole deck back through `parseDeck` before storing it. The agent's
  * full reference (with its patch ops) is `SLIDES_GUIDE` at the end of this file.
  * ===========================================================================
  *
  * The one source of truth for the deck body: the app (freebuff/web
  * components/workspace/slides re-exports it) and the server (packages/internal
  * freebuff-artifacts/kinds.ts) both validate with it. zod only: no React, no DOM.
  */

 export type Slide = z.infer<typeof slideSchema>

 export type DeckParseResult =
   | { ok: true; deck: Deck }
   | { ok: false; issues: Array<{ path: string; message: string }> }
 
 /** Validate an untrusted deck (from the agent or storage). Never throws. */
 export function parseDeck(input: unknown): DeckParseResult {
   const result = deckSchema.safeParse(input)
   if (result.success) return { ok: true, deck: result.data }
   return {
     ok: false,
     issues: result.error.issues.map((issue) => ({
       path: issue.path.map(String).join('.'),
       message: issue.message,
     })),
   }
 }

 Layouts (canvas 960x540 for 16:9, 720x540 for 4:3):
 - title: cover; big title, subtitle under it; body ignored.
 - section: divider between parts; title, optional subtitle.
 - title-body: title on top, body below (bullets, numbered lists, bold, links, tables).
 - two-column: title, then columns side by side. REQUIRES columns.
 - image: optional title, one picture, subtitle as caption. REQUIRES image.
 - chart: title, one chart, body as a one-line takeaway. REQUIRES chart.
 - quote: body is the quotation (Markdown), subtitle the attribution.
 - blank: only body, centred. Use sparingly.
 Text fields are optional; only the payload a layout needs (columns, image, chart) is required.
 
 Markdown is GitHub-flavoured: - bullets, 1. numbered, **bold**, *italic*, [links](https://...), \`code\`, pipe tables. Raw HTML shows as text. Keep a slide to about 6 bullets or one table of <= 6 rows; long text overflows, it does not shrink.
 image.src (and an image element's src): an uploaded "/_asset/<id>" ref. For any picture from the web, call import_artifact_image first and use the ref it returns (read_artifact lists the deck's assets). Never hotlink an https:// URL: readers see a placeholder instead of it (a share link always). No data:, http: or javascript: URLs.
 chart (an object, not a string): { "type": "bar" | "line" | "area", "title"?: string, "x": "<row key for the x axis>",
   "series": [{ "key": "<row key>", "label": "<legend>" }] (1-${LIMITS.chartSeries}, unique, not x),

+import { ARTIFACT_KINDS, type ArtifactKind } from './artifacts'
+import { QUOTE_LEAD_IN } from './artifact-quote-frame'
+
+export const ARTIFACT_MENTION_LABELS: Record<ArtifactKind, string> = {
+  doc: 'Docs',
+  slides: 'Slides',
+  sheet: 'Sheets',
+  data: 'Data',
+  whiteboard: 'Whiteboard',
+  design: 'Design',
+}
+
+export const ARTIFACT_MENTIONS: readonly {
+  kind: ArtifactKind
+  name: string
+  label: string
+  aliases: readonly string[]
+}[] = [
+  { kind: 'doc', name: 'docs', label: 'Docs', aliases: ['doc', 'docs', 'document', 'documents'] },
+  { kind: 'slides', name: 'slides', label: 'Slides', aliases: ['slide', 'slides', 'deck', 'presentation', 'presentations'] },
+  { kind: 'sheet', name: 'sheets', label: 'Sheets', aliases: ['sheet', 'sheets', 'spreadsheet', 'spreadsheets', 'excel'] },
+  { kind: 'data', name: 'data', label: 'Data', aliases: ['data', 'dashboard', 'dashboards'] },
+  { kind: 'whiteboard', name: 'whiteboard', label: 'Whiteboard', aliases: ['whiteboard', 'whiteboards', 'board'] },
+  { kind: 'design', name: 'design', label: 'Design', aliases: ['design', 'designs'] },
+]
+
+export type ArtifactMention = {
+  start: number
+  end: number
+  text: string
+  kind: ArtifactKind
+  label: string
+}
+
+/** The selected format is carried by the same prompt through drafts, queues and history. */
+export function artifactMention(kind: ArtifactKind): string {
+  return `@[${ARTIFACT_MENTION_LABELS[kind]}](artifact:${kind})`
+}
+
+/** Raw token ranges for composer and message chips. Tokens do not grant artifact access. */
+export function readArtifactMentions(text: string): ArtifactMention[] {
+  const mentions: ArtifactMention[] = []
+  for (const match of text.matchAll(/@\[(Docs|Slides|Sheets|Data|Whiteboard|Design)\]\(artifact:(doc|slides|sheet|data|whiteboard|design)\)/g)) {
+    const kind = match[2] as ArtifactKind
+    if (match[1] !== ARTIFACT_MENTION_LABELS[kind]) continue
+    mentions.push({ start: match.index, end: match.index + match[0].length, text: match[0], kind, label: match[1] })
+  }
+  return mentions
+}
+
+/** Format choices in the user's prose, excluding quoted content and Markdown code examples. */
+export function artifactMentionKinds(prompt: string, allowed: readonly ArtifactKind[] = ARTIFACT_KINDS): ArtifactKind[] {
+  const kinds = new Set<ArtifactKind>()
+  let fence: { marker: string; length: number } | null = null
+  let inlineTicks = 0
+  for (const line of prompt.split(/\r?\n/)) {
+    const marker = /^ {0,3}(`{3,}|~{3,})/.exec(line)?.[1]
+    if (fence) {
+      if (marker?.[0] === fence.marker && marker.length >= fence.length && /^ {0,3}(?:`+|~+)[ \t]*$/.test(line)) fence = null
+      continue
+    }
+    if (/^(?: {0,3}>| {4}|\t)/.test(line) || line.trimStart().startsWith(QUOTE_LEAD_IN)) continue
+    if (marker) {
+      fence = { marker: marker[0], length: marker.length }
+      continue
+    }
+    let prose = ''
+    for (const part of line.split(/(`+)/)) {
+      if (/^`+$/.test(part)) {
+        if (!inlineTicks) inlineTicks = part.length
+        else if (inlineTicks === part.length) inlineTicks = 0
+        prose += ' '.repeat(part.length)
+      } else prose += inlineTicks ? ' '.repeat(part.length) : part
+    }
+    for (const mention of readArtifactMentions(prose)) {
+      const escapes = /\\+$/.exec(prose.slice(0, mention.start))?.[0].length ?? 0
+      if (escapes % 2) continue
+      if (allowed.includes(mention.kind)) kinds.add(mention.kind)
+    }
+  }
+  return [...kinds]
+}

 // Whiteboard: elements keyed by id, each merged key by key (a move on one side and a text edit on
 // the other both land); the list order is the layer order. Deleting an element removes the arrows
 // bound to it and detaches its stickers (`deleteElements` in whiteboard-ops.ts), so before merging
 // each side's clean deletions are replayed onto the other side's elements, and after merging a
 // sweep drops any arrow still bound to a missing element and detaches any orphaned sticker.
 
 export const boundId = (end: unknown): string | null => (isObj(end) && typeof end.elementId === 'string' ? end.elementId : null)
 
 /** `elements` with arrows bound to `gone` removed and stickers attached to `gone` detached. */
 export function withoutBoundTo(elements: unknown[], gone: (id: string) => boolean): unknown[] {
   const out: unknown[] = []
   for (const element of elements) {
     if (!isObj(element)) {
       out.push(element)
       continue
     }
     if (element.type === 'arrow') {
       const [from, to] = [boundId(element.from), boundId(element.to)]
       if ((from !== null && gone(from)) || (to !== null && gone(to))) continue
     }
     if (element.type === 'sticker' && typeof element.attachedTo === 'string' && gone(element.attachedTo)) {
       const { attachedTo: _gone, ...rest } = element
       out.push(rest)
       continue
@@ -1210,52 +1217,60 @@ const mergeWhiteboard: JsonMerger = (base, mine, theirs, ctx) => {
       item: (id) => unit(`elements/${id}`, `element ${quote(id)}`),
       order: unit('elements:order', 'element order'),
       whole: unit('elements', 'elements'),
       // Key by key; a table's cells, merged cells and column / row weights only make sense
       // together (a merge or a weight names a row or column), so they are one unit.
       inner: (id) =>
         objectInner(unit(`elements/${id}`, `element ${quote(id)}`), { votes: votesField(unit(`elements/${id}`, `element ${quote(id)}`)) }, [
           ['rows', 'merges', 'colWidths', 'rowHeights'],
         ]),
     }),
   })
   if (Array.isArray(out.elements)) {
     const present = new Set(out.elements.map(idOf).filter((id): id is string => id !== null))
     out.elements = withoutBoundTo(out.elements, (id) => !present.has(id))
   }
   return out
 }

   keyedListField({

 // Design: boards keyed by id; their nodes merged as ONE map across the whole design (ids are
 // unique design-wide, so a node may move between boards). Each node is its own props (everything
 // but `children`), where it sits (its parent: a frame, or a board for a root), and, for a frame,
 // the order of its children. A node is placed under its merged parent, so a move on one side and
 // an edit on the other both land; the trees are rebuilt from the merged map. A node no tree
 // reaches any more (two moves forming a cycle) is a conflict rather than a silent loss.
 
 type FlatNode = { props: Obj; kids: string[] | null; parent: string }
 type FlatDesign = { boards: Obj[]; nodes: Map<string, FlatNode>; boardOf: Map<string, string> }
 
 class NotFlat extends Error {}
 
 const boardParent = (boardId: string) => `board:${boardId}`
 
 function flattenDesign(design: Obj): FlatDesign {
   const nodes = new Map<string, FlatNode>()
   const boardOf = new Map<string, string>()
   if (design.boards !== undefined && !Array.isArray(design.boards)) throw new NotFlat()

 const ANIMALS = [
   'Otter', 'Fox', 'Heron', 'Lynx', 'Panda', 'Koala', 'Badger', 'Falcon', 'Moose', 'Gecko',
   'Walrus', 'Bison', 'Ibis', 'Marten', 'Puffin', 'Quokka', 'Raven', 'Seal', 'Tapir', 'Yak',

-/** FNV-1a gives each peer id a stable display color. */
+/** FNV-1a: a small stable hash of a seed (a peer id), used only to pick a colour. */
 function hash(seed: string) {
   let h = 0x811c9dc5
   for (let i = 0; i < seed.length; i++) {
     h ^= seed.charCodeAt(i)
     h = Math.imul(h, 0x01000193)
   }
   return h >>> 0
 }
 
 export const artifactPeerColor = (seed: string) => ARTIFACT_PEER_COLORS[hash(seed) % ARTIFACT_PEER_COLORS.length]!
 export const anonymousPeerLabel = (seed: string) => `Anonymous ${ANIMALS[hash(`a:${seed}`) % ANIMALS.length]}`
 
 /** Two-letter initials for an avatar. */
 export function peerInitials(name: string): string {
   const words = name.replace(/^Anonymous\s+/, '').trim().split(/\s+/).filter(Boolean)
   if (!words.length) return '?'
   const letters = words.length === 1 ? words[0]!.slice(0, 2) : `${words[0]![0]}${words[words.length - 1]![0]}`
   return letters.toUpperCase()
 }

 /**
  * Freebuff artifacts: hosted, agent-authored things a user keeps, edits and
  * shares by link (a doc, a deck, a sheet...). The wire contract between the
  * workspace UI and `/api/v1/cloud/artifacts` (web/src/server/desktop-cloud/
  * artifacts-api.ts). The body formats and their validation live in
  * packages/internal/src/freebuff-artifacts/kinds.ts.
  *
  * Routes, all under `/api/v1/cloud/` (the workspace gateway serves the same
  * paths under `/api/workspace/cloud/`):
  *  - `GET  artifacts?scope=mine|shared&kind=&search=&limit=&sort=` → `ArtifactListResponse` (pinned first)
  *  - `GET  artifacts/:id/head?linkToken=` → `ArtifactHeadResponse` (the open tab's poll)
  *  - `PUT  artifacts/:id/pin` `ArtifactPinRequest` → `{ pinned }`
  *  - `PUT  artifacts/:id/template` `ArtifactTemplateRequest` → `ArtifactTemplateResponse` (owner only)
  *  - `POST artifacts` `ArtifactCreateRequest` → `ArtifactResponse`
  *  - `GET  artifacts/:id` → `ArtifactResponse`
+ *  - `GET  artifacts/:id?metadata=1` → `ArtifactLabelResponse` (a restored tab's label)
  *  - `PUT  artifacts/:id` `ArtifactSaveRequest` → `ArtifactResponse`, or 409 `ArtifactConflict`
  *  - `PATCH artifacts/:id` `ArtifactRenameRequest` → `{ artifact }`
  *  - `DELETE artifacts/:id` → `{ deleted: true }`
  *  - `GET  artifacts/:id/share` → `ArtifactAccess`
  *  - `POST artifacts/:id/share` `ArtifactShareRequest` → `ArtifactShareResponse`
  *  - `POST artifacts/:id/invites` `ArtifactInviteRequest` → `{ invite }`
  *  - `DELETE artifacts/:id/invites/:inviteId` → `{ revoked: true }`
  *  - `GET  artifacts/shared/:token` (no sign-in; a share link's or an invite email's token) → `ArtifactResponse` with `via`
  *  - `GET  artifacts/:id/revisions?before=&limit=&linkToken=` → `ArtifactRevisionListResponse`
  *  - `GET  artifacts/:id/revisions/:rev?linkToken=` → `ArtifactRevisionResponse`
  *  - `POST artifacts/:id/revisions/:rev/restore` `ArtifactRestoreRequest` → `ArtifactResponse`, or 409 `ArtifactConflict`
  *  - `GET  artifacts/:id/comments?state=open|resolved|all&linkToken=` → `ArtifactCommentListResponse`
  *  - `POST artifacts/:id/comments` `ArtifactCommentAddRequest` → `{ thread: ArtifactCommentThread }`
  *  - `PATCH artifacts/:id/comments/:commentId` `ArtifactCommentUpdateRequest` → `{ thread: ArtifactCommentThread }`
  *  - `DELETE artifacts/:id/comments/:commentId?linkToken=` → `{ deleted: true }`
  *  - `POST artifacts/:id/assets?linkToken=` raw image bytes → `{ asset: ArtifactAsset }` (editors, signed in)
  *  - `GET  artifacts/:id/assets?linkToken=` → `ArtifactAssetListResponse`
  *  - `GET  artifacts/:id/assets/:assetId?linkToken=` → the image bytes (anyone who can read)
  *  - `GET  artifacts/shared/:token/assets/:assetId` (no sign-in) → the image bytes
  *  - `POST artifacts/shared/:token/accept` (signed in) → `ArtifactInviteAcceptResponse`, or 403 `ArtifactInviteRefusal`
  *  - `POST artifacts/:id/invites/:inviteId/email` (server to server only;
  *    the workspace gateway never forwards it) → `ArtifactInviteEmailClaim`
  *
  * The browser asks freebuff-web's `POST /api/artifacts/invite-email`
  * (`ArtifactInviteEmailRequest` → `ArtifactInviteEmailResponse`) to email an
  * invite it just created; see that route for the flow.
  *
  * A signed-in link holder presents the link token: in the JSON body of a
  * write, or as the `linkToken` query parameter of a GET or DELETE.
  *
  * Who may do what (`artifactCan`): a viewer reads the artifact and its
  * comments; a commenter also comments and resolves threads; an editor also
  * saves, reads the version history and restores a version; the owner also
  * shares, deletes and deletes anyone's comment. Writing a comment needs a
  * signed-in account, never a link alone.
  */
